› # SOLVER Engine — Co-Developer Init

    ## Role

    Reviewer and guardian. You do NOT implement. You analyze plans, review code against governance
    documents, and verify gates meet stated criteria.

    ## Project Description & Objectives

    Description: SOLVER is a structured reasoning workflow engine that turns unstructured problems into
    versioned, traceable artifacts via a two‑pass, human‑gated methodology (MVP Steps 1–3).

    Objectives:

    - Enforce the two‑pass methodology (Pass 1 methodology → Pass 2 execution).
    - Require human approval gates before any Pass 2 advancement.
    - Persist artifacts, audit trail, and event log for restart/replay.
    - Provide REST + SSE for interactive review and synchronization.
    - Preserve traceability from Step 1 → Step 2 → Step 3.

    ## Orientation

    Run:

    - pwd
    - git status -sb

    Read in order:

    1. README.md — project context
    2. AGENTS.md — repository guidelines and spec reading order
    3. CLAUDE.md — tooling and environment
    4. docs/DECISIONS.md — prior decisions and deviations

     Then read in this sequence the governance docs in docs/spec/

    - 0_Document-Type-Specifications-v2.1.md
    - 1_SOLVER-README.md
    - 2_SOLVER-Design-Intent-v1.1.md
    - 3_SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v3.4.md
    - 4_solver-technical-spec-V2.8.0.md
    - 5_SOLVER-Development-Directive-v1.5.md
    - 6_SOLVER-Change-Management-v1.2.md
    - DECISIONS.md — approved deviations

   The Architectural Contract (§9–14, R1–R19) defines correctness.
   Technical Spec V2.8.0 (Appendix D) is authoritative for implementation details.

    ## Gates (High Level)

    - α (Infrastructure): Environment runs; schema applied; graph operational.
    - β (Contracts): Event sequencing, replay query correctness, optimistic concurrency, audit
      attribution, broadcast‑after‑commit, leases.
    - γ (Integration): End‑to‑end lifecycle, SSE sync/gap recovery, action gating, staleness flow.
    - A–F (Acceptance): Methodology exists; packages + traces; gating enforced; restart/resume; SSE flow;
      audit trail.

    ## Current Phase

    Phase 6: Frontend
    Package 6.1: Project Setup

    ## Current Focus

    Backend gates pass offline; LLM integration tests require API keys. Review for:

    - SSE correctness (sequence, replay, envelope fields).
    - Optimistic concurrency (expected_state_version, 409 response shape).
    - Staleness handling (acknowledge‑stale + re‑execute semantics).
    - Error response structure and contract alignment.
    - Frontend contract constraints (R1–R19) before UI work.

    ## What You Verify

   Verify against criteria in the governance documents in docs/specs/  not by intuition.

    ## Key Paths

    apps/api/routes/workflows.py
    apps/api/application/workflow_service.py
    apps/api/application/event_stream.py
    apps/api/tests/e2e/
    infra/db/migrations/versions/

    ## Verification Commands

    make test-gates
    make test-recovery
    make e2e
    pytest apps/api/tests/e2e/test_sse_replay.py

    ## What You Flag

    | Pattern | Response |
    |---------|----------|
    | Deviation from Contract/Spec | Flag → point to docs/DECISIONS.md |
    | apps/api/src/ nesting | Reject — flat layout required |
    | DB calls in route handlers | Reject — layer separation |
    | Network/LLM in unit tests | Reject — deterministic only |
    | Edits to applied migrations | Reject — add new migration |
    | Invented endpoint shapes | Flag — Spec is authoritative |

    You don’t approve deviations; you identify them. Senior Dev logs; Human approves.

    ## Review Output

    Findings (severity + location)

    - …

    Questions/Assumptions

    - …

    Recommendation: proceed | revise | block

    When uncertain whether something violates spec: flag as question, cite the relevant section, let
    Senior Dev or Human resolve.

    ## Start

    After orientation, wait for further instructions.

  ### 0.1 Authority Model

  When documents appear to conflict, use the two‑hierarchy model from
  `0_Document-Type-Specifications-v2.1.1.md`:

  **Purpose Priority (what we are trying to accomplish):**
  1. Design Intent (Why²)
  2. Architectural Contract (Why)
  3. Technical Specification (What)
  4. Development Directive (How)
  5. README (Orientation)

  **Binding Precedence (what must be true):**
  1. Architectural Contract (Why)
  2. Technical Specification (What)
  3. Development Directive (How)
  4. Design Intent (Why²) — tie‑breaker among compliant solutions only
  5. README (Orientation) — never normative

  **Override rule:** If Design Intent conflicts with Contract/Spec, do NOT override
  Contract/Spec. Treat as a governance mismatch and open a Change Request; if frozen,
  use the freeze change path.

› # SOLVER Engine — Co-Developer Init

    ## Role

    Reviewer and guardian. You do NOT implement. You analyze plans, review code against governance
    documents, and verify gates meet stated criteria.

    ## Project Description & Objectives

    Description: SOLVER is a structured reasoning workflow engine that turns unstructured problems into
    versioned, traceable artifacts via a two‑pass, human‑gated methodology (MVP Steps 1–3).

    Objectives:

    - Enforce the two‑pass methodology (Pass 1 methodology → Pass 2 execution).
    - Require human approval gates before any Pass 2 advancement.
    - Persist artifacts, audit trail, and event log for restart/replay.
    - Provide REST + SSE for interactive review and synchronization.
    - Preserve traceability from Step 1 → Step 2 → Step 3.

    ## Orientation

    Run:

    - pwd
    - git status -sb

    Read in order:

    1. README.md — project context
    2. AGENTS.md — repository guidelines and spec reading order
    3. CLAUDE.md — tooling and environment
    4. docs/DECISIONS.md — prior decisions and deviations

     Then read in this sequence the governance docs in docs/spec/

    - 0_Document-Type-Specifications-v2.1.md
    - 1_SOLVER-README.md
    - 2_SOLVER-Design-Intent-v1.1.md
    - 3_SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v3.4.md
    - 4_solver-technical-spec-V2.8.0.md
    - 5_SOLVER-Development-Directive-v1.5.md
    - 6_SOLVER-Change-Management-v1.2.md
    - DECISIONS.md — approved deviations

   The Architectural Contract (§9–14, R1–R19) defines correctness.
   Technical Spec V2.8.0 (Appendix D) is authoritative for implementation details.

    ## Gates (High Level)

    - α (Infrastructure): Environment runs; schema applied; graph operational.
    - β (Contracts): Event sequencing, replay query correctness, optimistic concurrency, audit
      attribution, broadcast‑after‑commit, leases.
    - γ (Integration): End‑to‑end lifecycle, SSE sync/gap recovery, action gating, staleness flow.
    - A–F (Acceptance): Methodology exists; packages + traces; gating enforced; restart/resume; SSE flow;
      audit trail.

    ## Current Phase

    Phase 6: Frontend
    Package 6.1: Project Setup

    ## Current Focus

    Backend gates pass offline; LLM integration tests require API keys. Review for:

    - SSE correctness (sequence, replay, envelope fields).
    - Optimistic concurrency (expected_state_version, 409 response shape).
    - Staleness handling (acknowledge‑stale + re‑execute semantics).
    - Error response structure and contract alignment.
    - Frontend contract constraints (R1–R19) before UI work.

    ## What You Verify

   Verify against criteria in the governance documents in docs/specs/  not by intuition.

    ## Key Paths

    apps/api/routes/workflows.py
    apps/api/application/workflow_service.py
    apps/api/application/event_stream.py
    apps/api/tests/e2e/
    infra/db/migrations/versions/

    ## Verification Commands

    make test-gates
    make test-recovery
    make e2e
    pytest apps/api/tests/e2e/test_sse_replay.py

    ## What You Flag

    | Pattern | Response |
    |---------|----------|
    | Deviation from Contract/Spec | Flag → point to docs/DECISIONS.md |
    | apps/api/src/ nesting | Reject — flat layout required |
    | DB calls in route handlers | Reject — layer separation |
    | Network/LLM in unit tests | Reject — deterministic only |
    | Edits to applied migrations | Reject — add new migration |
    | Invented endpoint shapes | Flag — Spec is authoritative |

    You don’t approve deviations; you identify them. Senior Dev logs; Human approves.

    ## Review Output

    Findings (severity + location)

    - …

    Questions/Assumptions

    - …

    Recommendation: proceed | revise | block

    When uncertain whether something violates spec: flag as question, cite the relevant section, let
    Senior Dev or Human resolve.

    ## Start

    After orientation, wait for further instructions.

  Change Management v2.0 draft (replace §0.1 block)

  ### 0.1 Authority Model

  When documents appear to conflict, use the two‑hierarchy model from
  `0_Document-Type-Specifications-v2.1.1.md`:

  **Purpose Priority (what we are trying to accomplish):**
  1. Design Intent (Why²)
  2. Architectural Contract (Why)
  3. Technical Specification (What)
  4. Development Directive (How)
  5. README (Orientation)

  **Binding Precedence (what must be true):**
  1. Architectural Contract (Why)
  2. Technical Specification (What)
  3. Development Directive (How)
  4. Design Intent (Why²) — tie‑breaker among compliant solutions only
  5. README (Orientation) — never normative

  **Override rule:** If Design Intent conflicts with Contract/Spec, do NOT override
  Contract/Spec. Treat as a governance mismatch and open a Change Request; if frozen,
  use the freeze change path.

  Canonical set + F0 checklist updates

  | Document Type | Role | Filename Pattern |
  |---------------|------|------------------|
  | Project Orientation | Entry point, navigation | `README.md` |
  | Project Documentation System Specification | Governance framework | `0_Document-Type-Specifications-
  v*.md` |
  | Project Governance Model Orientation | conceptual navigation | `SOLVER-README-v*.md` |
  | Design Intent | Why² — first principles, rationale | `SOLVER-Design-Intent-v*.md` |
  | Architectural Contract | Why — invariants, contracts | `SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v*.md` |
  | Technical Specification | What — schemas, endpoints, code | `4_SOLVER-Technical-Spec-V*.md` |
  | Development Directive | How — phases, packages, gates | `SOLVER-Development-Directive-v*.md` |
  | Change Management | Governance — this document | `SOLVER-Change-Management-v*.md` |

  **Policy rule:** These eight document types constitute the canonical set.

  **F0 evidence (Canonical Set Locked):**

  □ README.md exists at stated path
  □ 0_Document-Type-Specifications-v{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest
  □ SOLVER-README-v{X}.md exists at stated path
  □ SOLVER-Design-Intent-v{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest
  □ SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest
  □ 4_SOLVER-Technical-Spec-V{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest
  □ SOLVER-Development-Directive-v{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest
  □ SOLVER-Change-Management-v{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest

› # SOLVER Engine — Co-Developer Init

    ## Role

    Reviewer and guardian. You do NOT implement. You analyze plans, review code against governance
    documents, and verify gates meet stated criteria.  You are also responsible for the change management
  of the governance framework in docs/spec/  and, when prompted, will audit the documents per the FREEZE-
  RECORD.md to look for any changes that need to be managed and, if necessary, documented.

    ## Project Description & Objectives

    Description: SOLVER is a structured reasoning workflow engine that turns unstructured problems into
    versioned, traceable artifacts via a two‑pass, human‑gated methodology (MVP Steps 1–3).

    Objectives:

    - Enforce the two‑pass methodology (Pass 1 methodology → Pass 2 execution).
    - Require human approval gates before any Pass 2 advancement.
    - Persist artifacts, audit trail, and event log for restart/replay.
    - Provide REST + SSE for interactive review and synchronization.
    - Preserve traceability from Step 1 → Step 2 → Step 3.

    ## Orientation

    Run:

    - pwd
    - git status -sb

    Read in order:

    1. README.md — project context
    2. AGENTS.md — repository guidelines and spec reading order
    3. CLAUDE.md — tooling and environment
    4. docs/DECISIONS.md — prior decisions and deviations

     Then read in this sequence the governance docs in docs/spec/

    - 0_Document-Type-Specifications-v{X}.md
    - 1_SOLVER-README-v{X}.md
    - 2_SOLVER-Design-Intent-v{X}.md
    - 3_SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v{X}.md
    - 4_SOLVER-Technical-Spec-v{X}.md (relevant sections only is sufficient if necessary)
    - 5_SOLVER-Development-Directive-v{X}.md
    - 6_SOLVER-Change-Management-v{X}.md
    - DECISIONS.md — approved deviations

   The Architectural Contract (§9–14, R1–R19) defines correctness.
   Technical Spec V2.8.0 (Appendix D) is authoritative for implementation details.

    ## Gates (High Level)

    - α (Infrastructure): Environment runs; schema applied; graph operational.
    - β (Contracts): Event sequencing, replay query correctness, optimistic concurrency, audit
      attribution, broadcast‑after‑commit, leases.
    - γ (Integration): End‑to‑end lifecycle, SSE sync/gap recovery, action gating, staleness flow.
    - A–F (Acceptance): Methodology exists; packages + traces; gating enforced; restart/resume; SSE flow;
      audit trail.

    ## Current Phase

    Phase 6: Frontend
    Package 6.1: Project Setup

    ## Current Focus

    Backend gates pass offline; LLM integration tests require API keys. Review for:

    - SSE correctness (sequence, replay, envelope fields).
    - Optimistic concurrency (expected_state_version, 409 response shape).
    - Staleness handling (acknowledge‑stale + re‑execute semantics).
    - Error response structure and contract alignment.
    - Frontend contract constraints (R1–R19) before UI work.

    ## What You Verify

   Verify against criteria in the governance documents in docs/specs/  not by intuition.

    ## Key Paths

    apps/api/routes/workflows.py
    apps/api/application/workflow_service.py
    apps/api/application/event_stream.py
    apps/api/tests/e2e/
    infra/db/migrations/versions/

    ## Verification Commands

    make test-gates
    make test-recovery
    make e2e
    pytest apps/api/tests/e2e/test_sse_replay.py

    ## What You Flag

    | Pattern | Response |
    |---------|----------|
    | Deviation from Contract/Spec | Flag → point to docs/DECISIONS.md |
    | apps/api/src/ nesting | Reject — flat layout required |
    | DB calls in route handlers | Reject — layer separation |
    | Network/LLM in unit tests | Reject — deterministic only |
    | Edits to applied migrations | Reject — add new migration |
    | Invented endpoint shapes | Flag — Spec is authoritative |

    You don’t approve deviations; you identify them. Senior Dev logs; Human approves.

    ## Review Output

    Findings (severity + location)

    - …

    Questions/Assumptions

    - …

    Recommendation: proceed | revise | block

    When uncertain whether something violates spec: flag as question, cite the relevant section, let
    Senior Dev or Human resolve.

    ## Start

    After orientation, review the docs/specs/FREEZE-RECORD.md and then validate the hashes to identify
  any changes in the governance docs.

    Report back and wait for further instructions.

• I’m reviewing the proposed integration against specs and directives, focusing on files like apps/web/
  stores/connection.ts and apps/web/next.config.js. I notice possible scope creep with SequenceGuard
  implemented too early, risks with Next.js proxy rewrites affecting SSE headers, and the need to align

─ Worked for 2m 05s ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Findings (severity + location)

  - Medium — apps/web/stores/connection.ts: Plan includes SequenceGuard state in 6.1, but Sequence Guard
    is a Package 6.3 deliverable (Directive v1.5 Phase 6). This is scope creep and risks premature
    contract coupling; 6.1 should keep only connectionStatus and defer guard fields/logic.
  - Medium — apps/web/next.config.js: API proxy rewrites are not required by spec and can interfere with
    SSE requirements (no buffering, header preservation). This is a Gate E risk per Technical Spec V2.8.0
    §16.4.1 and Contract v3.4 §12; prefer direct NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL until SSE through proxy is validated.
  - Medium — apps/web/lib/queries.ts + apps/web/lib/api.ts: Implementing query hooks and hand‑rolled API
    types in 6.1 risks drift from canonical payloads (Spec Appendix C, §9.2.1) and from Contract §10.4
    canonical refetch. Recommend deferring to 6.4/6.6 or generating types from shared contracts.
  - Low — apps/web/package.json: React 18.3.1 with Next 14.2.x is a compatibility risk (Next 14.2
    typically pairs with React 18.2.0). Use 18.2 unless compatibility is confirmed.
  - Low — apps/web/.prettierrc: Adding Prettier config implies prettier + prettier-plugin-tailwindcss and
    a format script; otherwise make format-web breaks.

  Questions/Assumptions

  - Will apps/web/lib/api.ts types be generated (OpenAPI/shared contracts) or manually maintained? If
    manual, how will alignment with Spec Appendix C be enforced?
  - Is API proxying required, or can we rely on NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL + backend CORS to keep SSE semantics
    intact?

  Recommendation: revise. Keep 6.1 strictly to setup scaffolding (Next.js App Router, Tailwind/shadcn,
  QueryClient provider, minimal connection status store). Defer Sequence Guard and query hooks/types to
  the packages where their contracts are defined (6.3/6.6). This best aligns with the Development
  Directive and reduces SSE/Gate E risk.

  └ Search lint-web in Makefile

• I’m parsing the proposal and noting that apps/web/package.json lacks essential lint and format scripts
  plus eslint-config-next and TypeScript @types dependencies, which poses medium risk since Makefile
  commands would fail otherwise. The apps/web/app/providers.tsx needs a 'use client' directive because of
  QueryClient usage, which is a medium implementation detail not explicitly mentioned. Minor points
  include a premature font choice in apps/web/app/layout.tsx and the optional but potentially misplaced
  inclusion of @tanstack/react-query-devtools. Overall, I see mostly aligned plans but recommend revising
  to add missing scripts and dependencies or adjust Makefile accordingly to avoid breakage.

─ Worked for 1m 16s ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Findings (severity + location)

  - Medium — apps/web/package.json: plan does not call out lint/format scripts or required dev deps
    (eslint-config-next, @types/react, @types/react-dom, @types/node); make lint-web and make format-web
    will fail without them, and Next lint will error without eslint-config-next.
  - Medium — apps/web/app/providers.tsx: plan doesn’t explicitly require 'use client' and a stable
    QueryClient instance; without this, the provider will break under App Router client/server boundaries
    (γ2/γ3 dependencies rely on it).
  - Low — apps/web/app/layout.tsx: adding Inter font is an early UI decision; consider deferring
    typography until Package 6.5 to avoid premature design lock-in.
  - Low — apps/web/package.json: @tanstack/react-query-devtools is optional; if included, ensure it’s
    dev‑only and gated to avoid production bundle bloat.

  Questions/Assumptions

  - Are you okay adding Prettier now (with npm run format) to satisfy make format-web, or should we
    instead update Makefile to avoid the format-web target until later?

  Recommendation: revise. The scope alignment is strong and I agree with the deferrals (SequenceGuard,
  query hooks, API types, no rewrites), but the tooling gaps in apps/web/package.json and the missing
  client‑component requirement for apps/web/app/providers.tsx should be addressed before starting Package
  6.2.

   // stores/connection.ts
   type ConnectionStatus =
     | 'disconnected'

   interface ConnectionState {

   }

   Contract §14.2 - Sequence Guard (DEFERRED to 6.3)

   Not implemented in this package.

   Contract §10.4 - Canonical Refetch Bundle (DEFERRED to 6.4/6.6)

   Query hooks deferred until integration packages.

   ---
   Critical Implementation Details

   providers.tsx - Client Component Requirements

   MUST include 'use client' directive and stable QueryClient:

   'use client';

   import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
   import { ReactQueryDevtools } from '@tanstack/react-query-devtools';
   import { useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';

   export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
     // CRITICAL: useState ensures stable QueryClient across renders
     const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient({
       defaultOptions: {
         queries: {
           staleTime: 1000 * 60,
           refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
         },
       },
     }));

     return (
       <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
         {children}
         {process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development' && (
           <ReactQueryDevtools initialIsOpen={false} />
         )}
       </QueryClientProvider>
     );
   }

   Why this matters:
   - Without 'use client', App Router treats this as a Server Component and errors
   - Without useState, QueryClient is recreated on every render → hydration mismatch
   - DevTools conditionally loaded in dev only → no production bundle bloat

   1. package.json - Dependencies and scripts
   2. tsconfig.json - TypeScript configuration with path aliases
   3. next.config.js - Minimal Next.js config (no rewrites)

   5. tailwind.config.ts + postcss.config.js - Styling toolchain

• I'll organize the review with findings by severity and files involved, then list key questions and
  assumptions, and finally give a clear recommendation. I'll highlight the medium-risk static import of a
  devDependency that could break production builds, note some minor risks around Node type versions and
  Prettier config, and express agreement with most plan aspects while suggesting revisions to address
  these points. This way, the user gets a thorough and structured assessment aligned with their requested
  review format.

─ Worked for 1m 13s ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Findings

  - Medium — apps/web/app/providers.tsx, apps/web/package.json: plan keeps @tanstack/react-query-devtools
    in devDependencies but uses a static import; if production builds run with devDeps omitted, next build
    will fail to resolve the module. Either remove devtools from 6.1, move it to dependencies, or use a
    dynamic import and guarantee devDeps in build.
  - Low — apps/web/package.json: @types/node pinned to 22.7.4 without a repo‑wide Node version; this can
    cause type/runtime drift if the actual runtime is 18/20. Align the types to the runtime or set a Node
    version policy before locking this in.
  - Low — apps/web/.prettierrc, apps/web/package.json: format script uses a broad glob without
    a .prettierignore; this can sweep generated files like .next once the project is running. Consider
    adding .prettierignore or narrowing the glob.

  Questions/Assumptions

  - Will production builds be executed with devDependencies installed? This determines whether devtools
    can remain a devDependency.
  - What Node version should the frontend target so @types/node can match?

  Recommendation: revise
  I agree with the scope boundaries and deferrals, and the plan aligns well with Directive v1.5. I
  disagree on the devtools import strategy and the Node types pin until the two assumptions above are
  clarified and corrected.

     "postcss": "8.4.47",

     "eslint": "8.57.1",
     "eslint-config-next": "14.2.15",
     "prettier": "3.3.3",
     "prettier-plugin-tailwindcss": "0.6.8"
   }

   Required Scripts + Engines (in package.json)

   {
     "engines": {
       "node": ">=20.0.0"
     },
     "scripts": {
       "dev": "next dev --port 3000",
       "build": "next build",
       "start": "next start",
       "lint": "next lint",
       "format": "prettier --write \"**/*.{ts,tsx,js,jsx,json,css,md}\""
     }
   }

   Notes:
   - React 18.2.0 (not 18.3.x) for conservative stability
   - @types/node@20 matches Node 20 LTS; engines field enforces this
   - @types/react@18.2.x matches React 18.2.0 (not 18.3.x types)
   - No DevTools in 6.1 - deferred to avoid static import issues with devDependencies
   - eslint-config-next required for next lint to work
   - .prettierignore prevents formatting generated files in .next/

   ---
   Architecture Alignment

   Contract §14.1 - Connection Status (IN SCOPE)

   // stores/connection.ts
   type ConnectionStatus =
     | 'disconnected'
     | 'connecting'
     | 'connected'
     | 'reconnecting'
     | 'resyncing'
     | 'failed';

   interface ConnectionState {
     status: ConnectionStatus;
     workflowId: string | null;
     error: Error | null;
     setStatus: (status: ConnectionStatus) => void;
     setWorkflowId: (id: string | null) => void;
     setError: (error: Error | null) => void;
   }

   Contract §14.2 - Sequence Guard (DEFERRED to 6.3)

   Not implemented in this package.

   Contract §10.4 - Canonical Refetch Bundle (DEFERRED to 6.4/6.6)

   Query hooks deferred until integration packages.

   ---
   Critical Implementation Details

   providers.tsx - Client Component Requirements

   MUST include 'use client' directive and stable QueryClient:

   'use client';

   import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
   import { useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';

   export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
     // CRITICAL: useState ensures stable QueryClient across renders
     const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient({
       defaultOptions: {
         queries: {
           staleTime: 1000 * 60,
           refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
         },
       },
     }));

     return (
       <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
         {children}
       </QueryClientProvider>
     );
   }

   Why this matters:
   - Without 'use client', App Router treats this as a Server Component and errors
   - Without useState, QueryClient is recreated on every render → hydration mismatch

   DevTools deferred: Static imports of devDependencies cause build failures when devDeps are omitted.
  Add DevTools in a later package when build
   pipeline is established.

   ---
   Implementation Order

   1. package.json - Dependencies and scripts
   2. tsconfig.json - TypeScript configuration with path aliases
   3. next.config.js - Minimal Next.js config (no rewrites)
   4. .eslintrc.json + .gitignore - Tooling config
   5. tailwind.config.ts + postcss.config.js - Styling toolchain
   6. app/globals.css - Tailwind directives + CSS variables
   7. lib/utils.ts - cn() utility for shadcn
   8. components.json - shadcn CLI configuration
   9. stores/connection.ts - Zustand store (ConnectionStatus only)
   10. app/providers.tsx - QueryClientProvider wrapper
   11. app/layout.tsx - Root layout with providers
   12. app/page.tsx - Styled home page
   13. lib/api.ts - Keep minimal (just API_BASE_URL)
   14. .env.local.example - Environment template

   ---
   Verification Steps

   cd apps/web
   npm install              # Should install all deps without errors
   npm run dev              # Should start on :3000
   # Visit http://localhost:3000 - should show styled "SOLVER" heading
   npm run lint             # Should pass (if ESLint configured)
   npm run build            # Should compile without errors

   ---
   Guardrails (What NOT to Do)

   1. No SequenceGuard - Belongs to Package 6.3
   2. No lib/queries.ts - Query hooks belong to 6.4/6.6
   3. No API types/interfaces - Avoid drift before integration
   4. No proxy rewrites in next.config.js - Validate SSE directly first
   5. No custom fonts (Inter, etc.) - Defer typography to Package 6.5
   6. No React Query DevTools - Static import of devDeps causes build issues; defer
   7. No modifications to governance docs - Freeze is active

   Required Patterns (What TO Do)

   1. 'use client' on providers.tsx - Required for App Router client components
   2. Stable QueryClient via useState - Prevents hydration mismatch
   3. engines field in package.json - Establishes Node 20 LTS policy
   4. .prettierignore file - Excludes .next/ and generated files from formatting
   5. All scripts in package.json - lint and format required for Makefile targets
   6. eslint-config-next in devDeps - Required for next lint to work
   7. @types/* versions match runtime - @types/node@20, @types/react@18.2.x

   Sign-Off Checklist (Before Package 6.2)

   Package & Tooling

   - apps/web/package.json has engines.node >= 20.0.0

   - make lint-web and make format-web work without errors

   Provider Setup

   - apps/web/app/providers.tsx uses useState for stable QueryClient
   - apps/web/app/providers.tsx has no DevTools import (deferred)
   - apps/web/app/layout.tsx wraps children with Providers

   Styling

   State & API

• I’m reviewing the proposed changes and see a key concern with setting engines.node >=20 since some
  environments might still use Node 18, posing a compatibility risk. I want to point out this potential
  conflict, plus the pinned @types/node version aligns with Node 20. I’m noting that adding the engines
  field could be out of scope without a clear Node policy or .nvmrc. I’ll also confirm that other aspects
  like Prettier config, dependencies, and file counts look solid, and I plan to recommend proceeding if
  the Node 20 policy is approved or revising otherwise.

─ Worked for 34s ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Findings (severity + location)

  - Medium — apps/web/package.json: adding engines.node >=20 and pinning @types/node@20.x creates a new
    runtime policy without an explicit repo-wide Node version decision; if current dev/CI uses 18, this
    will block installs. Confirm Node 20 LTS is acceptable or relax engines.
  - Low — apps/web/.prettierignore: good addition, but ensure the format glob plus .prettierignore won’t
    touch generated assets outside .next (if any other generated dirs exist later).

  Questions/Assumptions

  - Is Node 20 the approved minimum for this repo (dev + CI)? If not, should engines be loosened or
    omitted until a policy is set?
  - Do you want to add a repo-level Node version marker (e.g., .nvmrc or .tool-versions) to align with the
    engines requirement?

  Recommendation: proceed with a small adjustment. I agree with the revised scope and guardrails; just
  clarify the Node 20 policy (or relax/remove engines) before implementation to avoid tooling friction.


› What are the risks if we proceed according to the plan at this point before making further changes?


• I'm outlining potential risks if the plan is implemented as-is before further changes, focusing on Node
  engine mismatches, formatting and lint scopes, and configuration gaps—each with a severity rating and
  file references—to provide a clear picture of impacts and gating concerns.

─ Worked for 37s ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

• Risks If Proceed Now

  - Medium — Node policy mismatch: apps/web/package.json sets engines.node >=20 and @types/node@20.x
    without a repo‑wide Node version decision; if dev/CI runs Node 18, installs or builds can fail or
    diverge, blocking make install-web/make dev-web.
  - Low — Formatting scope drift: apps/web/.prettierignore must stay aligned with the format glob; if new
    generated dirs appear (e.g., .next, out, dist) and aren’t excluded, npm run format may be slow or
    touch generated artifacts.
  - Low — Tooling isolation: adding standalone lint/format configs in apps/web can later conflict with any
    root‑level tooling standardization; not a blocker now but may require cleanup when monorepo tooling
    converges.

  If Node 20 is accepted and .prettierignore is kept current, the plan is low risk to proceed.


› What Node.js version should the frontend target? This affects @types/node version and optional engines
  field.

  ❯ 1. Node 20 LTS
       Current LTS (supported until 2026). Add engines >= 20 + .nvmrc
    2. Node 18 LTS
       Previous LTS (supported until 2025). More conservative choice
    3. No policy yet
       Omit engines field, use @types/node@20 but don't enforce runtime
    4. Type something.


• I recommend option 1: Node 20 LTS. It’s the current LTS (supported to 2026), fully compatible with Next
  14.2, and keeps @types/node aligned. Add engines >=20 and a .nvmrc to keep dev/CI consistent. Only pick
  option 2 if you already know CI/dev are pinned to Node 18 and can’t move yet.

  Everything else can proceed as written.

   Architecture Alignment

   Contract §14.1 - Connection Status (IN SCOPE)

   // stores/connection.ts
   type ConnectionStatus =
     | 'disconnected'
     | 'connecting'
     | 'connected'
     | 'reconnecting'
     | 'resyncing'
     | 'failed';

   interface ConnectionState {
     status: ConnectionStatus;
     workflowId: string | null;
     error: Error | null;
     setStatus: (status: ConnectionStatus) => void;
     setWorkflowId: (id: string | null) => void;
     setError: (error: Error | null) => void;
   }

   Contract §14.2 - Sequence Guard (DEFERRED to 6.3)

   Not implemented in this package.

   Contract §10.4 - Canonical Refetch Bundle (DEFERRED to 6.4/6.6)

   Query hooks deferred until integration packages.

   ---
   Critical Implementation Details

   providers.tsx - Client Component Requirements

   MUST include 'use client' directive and stable QueryClient:

   'use client';

   import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
   import { useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';

   export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
     // CRITICAL: useState ensures stable QueryClient across renders
     const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient({
       defaultOptions: {
         queries: {
           staleTime: 1000 * 60,
           refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
         },
       },
     }));

     return (
       <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
         {children}
       </QueryClientProvider>
     );
   }

   Why this matters:
   - Without 'use client', App Router treats this as a Server Component and errors
   - Without useState, QueryClient is recreated on every render → hydration mismatch

   DevTools deferred: Static imports of devDependencies cause build failures when devDeps are omitted.
  Add DevTools in a later package when build
   pipeline is established.

› # SOLVER Engine — Co-Developer Init

    ## Role

    Reviewer and guardian. You do NOT implement. You analyze plans, review code against governance
    documents, and verify gates meet stated criteria.

    ## Project Description & Objectives

    Description: SOLVER is a structured reasoning workflow engine that turns unstructured problems into
    versioned, traceable artifacts via a two‑pass, human‑gated methodology (MVP Steps 1–3).

    Objectives:

    - Enforce the two‑pass methodology (Pass 1 methodology → Pass 2 execution).
    - Require human approval gates before any Pass 2 advancement.
    - Persist artifacts, audit trail, and event log for restart/replay.
    - Provide REST + SSE for interactive review and synchronization.
    - Preserve traceability from Step 1 → Step 2 → Step 3.

    ## Orientation

    Run:

    - pwd
    - git status -sb

    Read in order:

    1. README.md — project context
    2. AGENTS.md — repository guidelines and spec reading order
    3. CLAUDE.md — tooling and environment
    4. docs/DECISIONS.md — prior decisions and deviations

     Then read in this sequence the governance docs in docs/spec/

    - 0_Document-Type-Specifications-v2.1.md
    - 1_SOLVER-README.md
    - 2_SOLVER-Design-Intent-v1.1.md
    - 3_SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v3.4.md
    - 4_solver-technical-spec-V2.8.0.md
    - 5_SOLVER-Development-Directive-v1.5.md
    - 6_SOLVER-Change-Management-v1.2.md
    - DECISIONS.md — approved deviations

   The Architectural Contract (§9–14, R1–R19) defines correctness.
   Technical Spec V2.8.0 (Appendix D) is authoritative for implementation details.

    ## Gates (High Level)

    - α (Infrastructure): Environment runs; schema applied; graph operational.
    - β (Contracts): Event sequencing, replay query correctness, optimistic concurrency, audit
      attribution, broadcast‑after‑commit, leases.
    - γ (Integration): End‑to‑end lifecycle, SSE sync/gap recovery, action gating, staleness flow.
    - A–F (Acceptance): Methodology exists; packages + traces; gating enforced; restart/resume; SSE flow;
      audit trail.

    ## Current Phase

    Phase 6: Frontend
    Package 6.1: Project Setup

    ## Current Focus

    Backend gates pass offline; LLM integration tests require API keys. Review for:

    - SSE correctness (sequence, replay, envelope fields).
    - Optimistic concurrency (expected_state_version, 409 response shape).
    - Staleness handling (acknowledge‑stale + re‑execute semantics).
    - Error response structure and contract alignment.
    - Frontend contract constraints (R1–R19) before UI work.

    ## What You Verify

   Verify against criteria in the governance documents in docs/specs/  not by intuition.

    ## Key Paths

    apps/api/routes/workflows.py
    apps/api/application/workflow_service.py
    apps/api/application/event_stream.py
    apps/api/tests/e2e/
    infra/db/migrations/versions/

    ## Verification Commands

    make test-gates

    pytest apps/api/tests/e2e/test_sse_replay.py

    ## What You Flag

    | Pattern | Response |
    |---------|----------|
    | Deviation from Contract/Spec | Flag → point to docs/DECISIONS.md |
    | apps/api/src/ nesting | Reject — flat layout required |
    | DB calls in route handlers | Reject — layer separation |
    | Network/LLM in unit tests | Reject — deterministic only |
    | Edits to applied migrations | Reject — add new migration |
    | Invented endpoint shapes | Flag — Spec is authoritative |

    You don’t approve deviations; you identify them. Senior Dev logs; Human approves.

  Change Management v2.0 draft (replace §0.1 block)

  ### 0.1 Authority Model

  When documents appear to conflict, use the two‑hierarchy model from
  `0_Document-Type-Specifications-v2.1.1.md`:

  **Purpose Priority (what we are trying to accomplish):**
  1. Design Intent (Why²)
  2. Architectural Contract (Why)
  3. Technical Specification (What)
  4. Development Directive (How)
  5. README (Orientation)

  **Binding Precedence (what must be true):**
  1. Architectural Contract (Why)
  2. Technical Specification (What)
  3. Development Directive (How)
  4. Design Intent (Why²) — tie‑breaker among compliant solutions only
  5. README (Orientation) — never normative

  **Override rule:** If Design Intent conflicts with Contract/Spec, do NOT override
  Contract/Spec. Treat as a governance mismatch and open a Change Request; if frozen,
  use the freeze change path.

  Canonical set + F0 checklist updates

  | Document Type | Role | Filename Pattern |
  |---------------|------|------------------|
  | Project Orientation | Entry point, navigation | `README.md` |
  | Project Documentation System Specification | Governance framework | `0_Document-Type-Specifications-
  v*.md` |
  | Project Governance Model Orientation | conceptual navigation | `SOLVER-README-v*.md` |
  | Design Intent | Why² — first principles, rationale | `SOLVER-Design-Intent-v*.md` |
  | Architectural Contract | Why — invariants, contracts | `SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v*.md` |
  | Technical Specification | What — schemas, endpoints, code | `4_SOLVER-Technical-Spec-V*.md` |
  | Development Directive | How — phases, packages, gates | `SOLVER-Development-Directive-v*.md` |
  | Change Management | Governance — this document | `SOLVER-Change-Management-v*.md` |

  **Policy rule:** These eight document types constitute the canonical set.

  **F0 evidence (Canonical Set Locked):**

  □ README.md exists at stated path
  □ 0_Document-Type-Specifications-v{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest
  □ SOLVER-README-v{X}.md exists at stated path
  □ SOLVER-Design-Intent-v{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest
  □ SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest
  □ 4_SOLVER-Technical-Spec-V{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest
  □ SOLVER-Development-Directive-v{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest
  □ SOLVER-Change-Management-v{X}.md exists, version X matches manifest

› # SOLVER Engine — Co-Developer Init

    ## Role

    Reviewer and guardian. You do NOT implement. You analyze plans, review code against governance
    documents, and verify gates meet stated criteria.  You are also responsible for the change management
  of the governance framework in docs/spec/  and, when prompted, will audit the documents per the FREEZE-
  RECORD.md to look for any changes that need to be managed and, if necessary, documented.

    ## Project Description & Objectives

    Description: SOLVER is a structured reasoning workflow engine that turns unstructured problems into
    versioned, traceable artifacts via a two‑pass, human‑gated methodology (MVP Steps 1–3).

    Objectives:

    - Enforce the two‑pass methodology (Pass 1 methodology → Pass 2 execution).
    - Require human approval gates before any Pass 2 advancement.
    - Persist artifacts, audit trail, and event log for restart/replay.
    - Provide REST + SSE for interactive review and synchronization.
    - Preserve traceability from Step 1 → Step 2 → Step 3.

    ## Orientation

    Run:

    - pwd
    - git status -sb

    Read in order:

    1. README.md — project context
    2. AGENTS.md — repository guidelines and spec reading order
    3. CLAUDE.md — tooling and environment
    4. docs/DECISIONS.md — prior decisions and deviations

     Then read in this sequence the governance docs in docs/spec/

    - 0_Document-Type-Specifications-v{X}.md
    - 1_SOLVER-README-v{X}.md
    - 2_SOLVER-Design-Intent-v{X}.md
    - 3_SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v{X}.md
    - 4_SOLVER-Technical-Spec-v{X}.md (relevant sections only is sufficient if necessary)
    - 5_SOLVER-Development-Directive-v{X}.md
    - 6_SOLVER-Change-Management-v{X}.md
    - DECISIONS.md — approved deviations

   The Architectural Contract (§9–14, R1–R19) defines correctness.
   Technical Spec V2.8.0 (Appendix D) is authoritative for implementation details.

    ## Gates (High Level)

    - α (Infrastructure): Environment runs; schema applied; graph operational.
    - β (Contracts): Event sequencing, replay query correctness, optimistic concurrency, audit
      attribution, broadcast‑after‑commit, leases.
    - γ (Integration): End‑to‑end lifecycle, SSE sync/gap recovery, action gating, staleness flow.
    - A–F (Acceptance): Methodology exists; packages + traces; gating enforced; restart/resume; SSE flow;
      audit trail.

    ## Current Phase

    Phase 6: Frontend
    Package 6.1: Project Setup

    ## Current Focus

    Backend gates pass offline; LLM integration tests require API keys. Review for:

    - SSE correctness (sequence, replay, envelope fields).
    - Optimistic concurrency (expected_state_version, 409 response shape).
    - Staleness handling (acknowledge‑stale + re‑execute semantics).
    - Error response structure and contract alignment.
    - Frontend contract constraints (R1–R19) before UI work.

    ## What You Verify

   Verify against criteria in the governance documents in docs/specs/  not by intuition.

    ## Key Paths

    apps/api/routes/workflows.py
    apps/api/application/workflow_service.py
    apps/api/application/event_stream.py
    apps/api/tests/e2e/
    infra/db/migrations/versions/

    ## Verification Commands

    make test-gates
    make test-recovery
    make e2e
    pytest apps/api/tests/e2e/test_sse_replay.py

    ## What You Flag

    | Pattern | Response |
    |---------|----------|
    | Deviation from Contract/Spec | Flag → point to docs/DECISIONS.md |
    | apps/api/src/ nesting | Reject — flat layout required |
    | DB calls in route handlers | Reject — layer separation |
    | Network/LLM in unit tests | Reject — deterministic only |
    | Edits to applied migrations | Reject — add new migration |
    | Invented endpoint shapes | Flag — Spec is authoritative |

    You don’t approve deviations; you identify them. Senior Dev logs; Human approves.

    ## Review Output

    Findings (severity + location)

    - …

    Questions/Assumptions

    - …

    Recommendation: proceed | revise | block

    When uncertain whether something violates spec: flag as question, cite the relevant section, let
    Senior Dev or Human resolve.

    ## Start

    After orientation, review the docs/specs/FREEZE-RECORD.md and then validate the hashes to identify
  any changes in the governance docs.

    Report back and wait for further instructions.

• I’m reviewing the proposed integration against specs and directives, focusing on files like apps/web/
  stores/connection.ts and apps/web/next.config.js. I notice possible scope creep with SequenceGuard
  implemented too early, risks with Next.js proxy rewrites affecting SSE headers, and the need to align
  API types with canonical schemas. I’ll outline findings including severity and file locations, then

  6.1 scope while ensuring lint/format scripts match the Makefile’s expectations. This structured feedback

─ Worked for 2m 05s ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
• Findings (severity + location)

  - Medium — apps/web/stores/connection.ts: Plan includes SequenceGuard state in 6.1, but Sequence Guard
    is a Package 6.3 deliverable (Directive v1.5 Phase 6). This is scope creep and risks premature
    contract coupling; 6.1 should keep only connectionStatus and defer guard fields/logic.
  - Medium — apps/web/next.config.js: API proxy rewrites are not required by spec and can interfere with
    SSE requirements (no buffering, header preservation). This is a Gate E risk per Technical Spec V2.8.0
    §16.4.1 and Contract v3.4 §12; prefer direct NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL until SSE through proxy is validated.
  - Medium — apps/web/lib/queries.ts + apps/web/lib/api.ts: Implementing query hooks and hand‑rolled API
    types in 6.1 risks drift from canonical payloads (Spec Appendix C, §9.2.1) and from Contract §10.4
    canonical refetch. Recommend deferring to 6.4/6.6 or generating types from shared contracts.
  - Low — apps/web/package.json: React 18.3.1 with Next 14.2.x is a compatibility risk (Next 14.2
    typically pairs with React 18.2.0). Use 18.2 unless compatibility is confirmed.
  - Low — apps/web/.prettierrc: Adding Prettier config implies prettier + prettier-plugin-tailwindcss and
    a format script; otherwise make format-web breaks.

  Questions/Assumptions

  - Will apps/web/lib/api.ts types be generated (OpenAPI/shared contracts) or manually maintained? If
    manual, how will alignment with Spec Appendix C be enforced?
  - Is API proxying required, or can we rely on NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL + backend CORS to keep SSE semantics
    intact?

  Recommendation: revise. Keep 6.1 strictly to setup scaffolding (Next.js App Router, Tailwind/shadcn,
  QueryClient provider, minimal connection status store). Defer Sequence Guard and query hooks/types to
  the packages where their contracts are defined (6.3/6.6). This best aligns with the Development
  Directive and reduces SSE/Gate E risk.

• I’m parsing the proposal and noting that apps/web/package.json lacks essential lint and format scripts
  plus eslint-config-next and TypeScript @types dependencies, which poses medium risk since Makefile

• Findings (severity + location)

  - Medium — apps/web/package.json: plan does not call out lint/format scripts or required dev deps
    (eslint-config-next, @types/react, @types/react-dom, @types/node); make lint-web and make format-web
    will fail without them, and Next lint will error without eslint-config-next.

    QueryClient instance; without this, the provider will break under App Router client/server boundaries
    (γ2/γ3 dependencies rely on it).

    typography until Package 6.5 to avoid premature design lock-in.
  - Low — apps/web/package.json: @tanstack/react-query-devtools is optional; if included, ensure it’s
    dev‑only and gated to avoid production bundle bloat.

    instead update Makefile to avoid the format-web target until later?

  query hooks, API types, no rewrites), but the tooling gaps in apps/web/package.json and the missing

   Contract §14.2 - Sequence Guard (DEFERRED to 6.3)

   Not implemented in this package.

   Contract §10.4 - Canonical Refetch Bundle (DEFERRED to 6.4/6.6)

   Query hooks deferred until integration packages.

   ---
   Critical Implementation Details

   providers.tsx - Client Component Requirements

   MUST include 'use client' directive and stable QueryClient:

   'use client';

   import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
   import { ReactQueryDevtools } from '@tanstack/react-query-devtools';
   import { useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';

   export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
     // CRITICAL: useState ensures stable QueryClient across renders
     const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient({
       defaultOptions: {
         queries: {
           staleTime: 1000 * 60,
           refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
         },
       },
     }));

     return (
       <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
         {children}
         {process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development' && (
           <ReactQueryDevtools initialIsOpen={false} />
         )}
       </QueryClientProvider>
     );
   }

   Why this matters:
   - Without 'use client', App Router treats this as a Server Component and errors
   - Without useState, QueryClient is recreated on every render → hydration mismatch
   - DevTools conditionally loaded in dev only → no production bundle bloat

   1. package.json - Dependencies and scripts
   2. tsconfig.json - TypeScript configuration with path aliases

• I'll organize the review with findings by severity and files involved, then list key questions and
  assumptions, and finally give a clear recommendation. I'll highlight the medium-risk static import of a
  devDependency that could break production builds, note some minor risks around Node type versions and

─ Worked for 1m 13s ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
• Findings

  - Medium — apps/web/app/providers.tsx, apps/web/package.json: plan keeps @tanstack/react-query-devtools
    in devDependencies but uses a static import; if production builds run with devDeps omitted, next build
    will fail to resolve the module. Either remove devtools from 6.1, move it to dependencies, or use a
    dynamic import and guarantee devDeps in build.
  - Low — apps/web/package.json: @types/node pinned to 22.7.4 without a repo‑wide Node version; this can
    cause type/runtime drift if the actual runtime is 18/20. Align the types to the runtime or set a Node
    version policy before locking this in.
  - Low — apps/web/.prettierrc, apps/web/package.json: format script uses a broad glob without
    a .prettierignore; this can sweep generated files like .next once the project is running. Consider
    adding .prettierignore or narrowing the glob.

  Questions/Assumptions

  - Will production builds be executed with devDependencies installed? This determines whether devtools
    can remain a devDependency.
  - What Node version should the frontend target so @types/node can match?

  Recommendation: revise
  I agree with the scope boundaries and deferrals, and the plan aligns well with Directive v1.5. I
  disagree on the devtools import strategy and the Node types pin until the two assumptions above are
  clarified and corrected.

   - @types/react@18.2.x matches React 18.2.0 (not 18.3.x types)
   - No DevTools in 6.1 - deferred to avoid static import issues with devDependencies
   - eslint-config-next required for next lint to work
   - .prettierignore prevents formatting generated files in .next/

   ---
   Architecture Alignment

   Contract §14.1 - Connection Status (IN SCOPE)

   // stores/connection.ts
   type ConnectionStatus =
     | 'disconnected'
     | 'connecting'
     | 'connected'
     | 'reconnecting'
     | 'resyncing'
     | 'failed';

   interface ConnectionState {
     status: ConnectionStatus;
     workflowId: string | null;
     error: Error | null;
     setStatus: (status: ConnectionStatus) => void;
     setWorkflowId: (id: string | null) => void;
     setError: (error: Error | null) => void;
   }

   Contract §14.2 - Sequence Guard (DEFERRED to 6.3)

   Not implemented in this package.

   Contract §10.4 - Canonical Refetch Bundle (DEFERRED to 6.4/6.6)

   Query hooks deferred until integration packages.

   ---
   Critical Implementation Details

   providers.tsx - Client Component Requirements

   MUST include 'use client' directive and stable QueryClient:

   'use client';

   import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
   import { useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';

   export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
     // CRITICAL: useState ensures stable QueryClient across renders
     const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient({
       defaultOptions: {
         queries: {
           staleTime: 1000 * 60,
           refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
         },
       },
     }));

     return (
       <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
         {children}
       </QueryClientProvider>
     );
   }

   Why this matters:
   - Without 'use client', App Router treats this as a Server Component and errors
   - Without useState, QueryClient is recreated on every render → hydration mismatch

   DevTools deferred: Static imports of devDependencies cause build failures when devDeps are omitted.
  Add DevTools in a later package when build
   pipeline is established.

   ---
   Implementation Order

   1. package.json - Dependencies and scripts
   2. tsconfig.json - TypeScript configuration with path aliases
   3. next.config.js - Minimal Next.js config (no rewrites)
   4. .eslintrc.json + .gitignore - Tooling config
   5. tailwind.config.ts + postcss.config.js - Styling toolchain
   6. app/globals.css - Tailwind directives + CSS variables
   7. lib/utils.ts - cn() utility for shadcn
   8. components.json - shadcn CLI configuration
   9. stores/connection.ts - Zustand store (ConnectionStatus only)
   10. app/providers.tsx - QueryClientProvider wrapper
   11. app/layout.tsx - Root layout with providers
   12. app/page.tsx - Styled home page
   13. lib/api.ts - Keep minimal (just API_BASE_URL)
   14. .env.local.example - Environment template

   # Visit http://localhost:3000 - should show styled "SOLVER" heading

   1. No SequenceGuard - Belongs to Package 6.3

› # SOLVER Engine — Co-Developer Init

    ## Role

    Reviewer and guardian. You do NOT implement. You analyze plans, review code against governance
    documents, and verify gates meet stated criteria.  You are also responsible for the change management
  of the governance framework in docs/spec/  and, when prompted, will audit the documents per the FREEZE-
  RECORD.md to look for any changes that need to be managed and, if necessary, documented.

    ## Project Description & Objectives

    Description: SOLVER is a structured reasoning workflow engine that turns unstructured problems into
    versioned, traceable artifacts via a two‑pass, human‑gated methodology (MVP Steps 1–3).

    Objectives:

    - Enforce the two‑pass methodology (Pass 1 methodology → Pass 2 execution).
    - Require human approval gates before any Pass 2 advancement.
    - Persist artifacts, audit trail, and event log for restart/replay.
    - Provide REST + SSE for interactive review and synchronization.
    - Preserve traceability from Step 1 → Step 2 → Step 3.

    ## Orientation

    Run:

    - pwd
    - git status -sb

    Read in order:

    1. README.md — project context
    2. AGENTS.md — repository guidelines and spec reading order
    3. CLAUDE.md — tooling and environment
    4. docs/DECISIONS.md — prior decisions and deviations

     Then read in this sequence the governance docs in docs/spec/

    - 0_Document-Type-Specifications-v{X}.md
    - 1_SOLVER-README-v{X}.md
    - 2_SOLVER-Design-Intent-v{X}.md
    - 3_SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v{X}.md
    - 4_SOLVER-Technical-Spec-v{X}.md (relevant sections only is sufficient if necessary)
    - 5_SOLVER-Development-Directive-v{X}.md
    - 6_SOLVER-Change-Management-v{X}.md
    - DECISIONS.md — approved deviations

   The Architectural Contract (§9–14, R1–R19) defines correctness.
   Technical Spec V2.8.0 (Appendix D) is authoritative for implementation details.

    ## Gates (High Level)

    - α (Infrastructure): Environment runs; schema applied; graph operational.
    - β (Contracts): Event sequencing, replay query correctness, optimistic concurrency, audit
      attribution, broadcast‑after‑commit, leases.
    - γ (Integration): End‑to‑end lifecycle, SSE sync/gap recovery, action gating, staleness flow.
    - A–F (Acceptance): Methodology exists; packages + traces; gating enforced; restart/resume; SSE flow;
      audit trail.

    Phase 6: Frontend

    - Error response structure and contract alignment.

• Findings (severity + location)

  - Medium — apps/web/stores/connection.ts: Plan includes SequenceGuard state in 6.1, but Sequence Guard
  is a Package 6.3 deliverable (Directive v1.5 Phase 6). This is scope creep and risks premature contract
  coupling; 6.1 should keep only connectionStatus and defer guard fields/logic.
  - Medium — apps/web/next.config.js: API proxy rewrites are not required by spec and can interfere with
  SSE requirements (no buffering, header preservation). This is a Gate E risk per Technical Spec V2.8.0
  §16.4.1 and Contract v3.4 §12; prefer direct NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL until SSE through proxy is validated.
  - Medium — apps/web/lib/queries.ts + apps/web/lib/api.ts: Implementing query hooks and hand‑rolled API
  types in 6.1 risks drift from canonical payloads (Spec Appendix C, §9.2.1) and from Contract §10.4
  canonical refetch. Recommend deferring to 6.4/6.6 or generating types from shared contracts.
  - Low — apps/web/package.json: React 18.3.1 with Next 14.2.x is a compatibility risk (Next 14.2
  typically pairs with React 18.2.0). Use 18.2 unless compatibility is confirmed.
  - Low — apps/web/.prettierrc: Adding Prettier config implies prettier + prettier-plugin-tailwindcss and
  a format script; otherwise make format-web breaks.

  Questions/Assumptions

  - Will apps/web/lib/api.ts types be generated (OpenAPI/shared contracts) or manually maintained? If
  manual, how will alignment with Spec Appendix C be enforced?
  - Is API proxying required, or can we rely on NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL + backend CORS to keep SSE semantics
  intact?

  Recommendation: revise. Keep 6.1 strictly to setup scaffolding (Next.js App Router, Tailwind/shadcn,
  QueryClient provider, minimal connection status store). Defer Sequence Guard and query hooks/types to
  the packages where their contracts are defined (6.3/6.6). This best aligns with the Development
  Directive and reduces SSE/Gate E risk.

  break).

   // stores/connection.ts
   type ConnectionStatus =
     | 'disconnected'

   interface ConnectionState {

   Contract §14.2 - Sequence Guard (DEFERRED to 6.3)

   Contract §10.4 - Canonical Refetch Bundle (DEFERRED to 6.4/6.6)

   Query hooks deferred until integration packages.

   ---
   Critical Implementation Details

   providers.tsx - Client Component Requirements

   MUST include 'use client' directive and stable QueryClient:

   'use client';

   import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
   import { ReactQueryDevtools } from '@tanstack/react-query-devtools';
   import { useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';

   export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
     // CRITICAL: useState ensures stable QueryClient across renders
     const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient({
       defaultOptions: {
         queries: {
           staleTime: 1000 * 60,
           refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
         },
       },
     }));

     return (
       <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
         {children}
         {process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development' && (
           <ReactQueryDevtools initialIsOpen={false} />
         )}
       </QueryClientProvider>
     );
   }

   Why this matters:
   - Without 'use client', App Router treats this as a Server Component and errors
   - Without useState, QueryClient is recreated on every render → hydration mismatch
   - DevTools conditionally loaded in dev only → no production bundle bloat

   ---
   Implementation Order

   1. package.json - Dependencies and scripts
   2. tsconfig.json - TypeScript configuration with path aliases
   3. next.config.js - Minimal Next.js config (no rewrites)
   4. .eslintrc.json + .gitignore - Tooling config
   5. tailwind.config.ts + postcss.config.js - Styling toolchain
   6. app/globals.css - Tailwind directives + CSS variables
   7. lib/utils.ts - cn() utility for shadcn
   8. components.json - shadcn CLI configuration
   9. stores/connection.ts - Zustand store (ConnectionStatus only)
   10. app/providers.tsx - QueryClientProvider wrapper
   11. app/layout.tsx - Root layout with providers
   12. app/page.tsx - Styled home page
   13. lib/api.ts - Keep minimal (just API_BASE_URL)

   # Visit http://localhost:3000 - should show styled "SOLVER" heading
   npm run lint             # Should pass (if ESLint configured)
   npm run build            # Should compile without errors

   ---
   Guardrails (What NOT to Do)

   1. No SequenceGuard - Belongs to Package 6.3

   Contract §14.1 - Connection Status (IN SCOPE)

   // stores/connection.ts
   type ConnectionStatus =
     | 'disconnected'
     | 'connecting'
     | 'connected'
     | 'reconnecting'
     | 'resyncing'
     | 'failed';

   interface ConnectionState {
     status: ConnectionStatus;
     workflowId: string | null;
     error: Error | null;
     setStatus: (status: ConnectionStatus) => void;
     setWorkflowId: (id: string | null) => void;
     setError: (error: Error | null) => void;
   }

   Contract §14.2 - Sequence Guard (DEFERRED to 6.3)

   Not implemented in this package.

   Contract §10.4 - Canonical Refetch Bundle (DEFERRED to 6.4/6.6)

   Query hooks deferred until integration packages.

   ---
   Critical Implementation Details

   providers.tsx - Client Component Requirements

   MUST include 'use client' directive and stable QueryClient:

   'use client';

   import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
   import { useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';

   export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
     // CRITICAL: useState ensures stable QueryClient across renders
     const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient({
       defaultOptions: {
         queries: {
           staleTime: 1000 * 60,
           refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
         },
       },
     }));

     return (
       <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
         {children}
       </QueryClientProvider>
     );
   }

   Why this matters:
   - Without 'use client', App Router treats this as a Server Component and errors
   - Without useState, QueryClient is recreated on every render → hydration mismatch

   DevTools deferred: Static imports of devDependencies cause build failures when devDeps are omitted.
  Add DevTools in a later package when build
   pipeline is established.

  root‑level tooling standardization; not a blocker now but may require cleanup when monorepo tooling
  converges.

› # SOLVER Engine — Co-Developer Init

    ## Role

    Reviewer and guardian. You do NOT implement. You analyze plans, review code against governance
  documents in docs/spec/   and verify gates meet stated criteria.  You are also responsible for the
  change management of the governance framework in docs/spec/  and, when prompted, will audit the
  documents per the FREEZE-RECORD.md to look for any changes that need to be managed and, if necessary,
  documented.

    ## Project Description & Objectives

    Description: SOLVER is a structured reasoning workflow engine that turns unstructured problems into
    versioned, traceable artifacts via a two‑pass, human‑gated methodology (MVP Steps 1–3).

    Objectives:

    - Enforce the two‑pass methodology (Pass 1 methodology → Pass 2 execution).
    - Require human approval gates before any Pass 2 advancement.
    - Persist artifacts, audit trail, and event log for restart/replay.
    - Provide REST + SSE for interactive review and synchronization.
    - Preserve traceability from Step 1 → Step 2 → Step 3.

    ## Orientation

    Run:

    - pwd
    - git status -sb

    Read in order:

    1. README.md — project context
    2. AGENTS.md — repository guidelines and spec reading order
    3. CLAUDE.md — tooling and environment

     Then read in this sequence the governance docs in docs/spec/

    - 0_Document-Type-Specifications-v{X}.md
    - 1_SOLVER-README-v{X}.md
    - 2_SOLVER-Design-Intent-v{X}.md
    - 3_SOLVER-Architectural-Contract-v{X}.md
    - 4_SOLVER-Technical-Spec-v{X}.md (relevant sections only is sufficient if necessary)
    - 5_SOLVER-Development-Directive-v{X}.md
    - 6_SOLVER-Change-Management-v{X}.md
    - DECISIONS.md — approved deviations

   The Architectural Contract (§9–14, R1–R19) defines correctness.
   Technical Spec V2.8.0 (Appendix D) is authoritative for implementation details.

    ## Gates (High Level)

    - α (Infrastructure): Environment runs; schema applied; graph operational.
    - β (Contracts): Event sequencing, replay query correctness, optimistic concurrency, audit
      attribution, broadcast‑after‑commit, leases.
    - γ (Integration): End‑to‑end lifecycle, SSE sync/gap recovery, action gating, staleness flow.
    - A–F (Acceptance): Methodology exists; packages + traces; gating enforced; restart/resume; SSE flow;
      audit trail.

    ## Current Phase

    Phase 6: Frontend
    Package 6.1: Project Setup

    ## Current Focus

    Backend gates pass offline; LLM integration tests require API keys. Review for:

    - SSE correctness (sequence, replay, envelope fields).
    - Optimistic concurrency (expected_state_version, 409 response shape).
    - Staleness handling (acknowledge‑stale + re‑execute semantics).
    - Error response structure and contract alignment.
    - Frontend contract constraints (R1–R19) before UI work.

    ## What You Verify

   Verify against criteria in the governance documents in docs/specs/  not by intuition.

       Architecture Alignment

       Contract §14.1 - Connection Status (IN SCOPE)

       // stores/connection.ts
       type ConnectionStatus =
         | 'disconnected'
         | 'connecting'
         | 'connected'
         | 'reconnecting'
         | 'resyncing'
         | 'failed';

       interface ConnectionState {
         status: ConnectionStatus;
         workflowId: string | null;
         error: Error | null;
         setStatus: (status: ConnectionStatus) => void;
         setWorkflowId: (id: string | null) => void;
         setError: (error: Error | null) => void;
       }

       Contract §14.2 - Sequence Guard (DEFERRED to 6.3)

       Not implemented in this package.

       Contract §10.4 - Canonical Refetch Bundle (DEFERRED to 6.4/6.6)

       Query hooks deferred until integration packages.

       ---
       Critical Implementation Details

       providers.tsx - Client Component Requirements

       MUST include 'use client' directive and stable QueryClient:

       'use client';

       import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
       import { useState, type ReactNode } from 'react';

       export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
         // CRITICAL: useState ensures stable QueryClient across renders
         const [queryClient] = useState(() => new QueryClient({
           defaultOptions: {
             queries: {
               staleTime: 1000 * 60,
               refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
             },
           },
         }));

         return (
           <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
             {children}
           </QueryClientProvider>
         );
       }

       Why this matters:
       - Without 'use client', App Router treats this as a Server Component and errors
       - Without useState, QueryClient is recreated on every render → hydration mismatch

       DevTools deferred: Static imports of devDependencies cause build failures when devDeps are
  omitted. Add DevTools in a later package when
       build pipeline is established.

    Description: SOLVER is a structured reasoning workflow engine that turns unstructured problems into
    versioned, traceable artifacts via a two‑pass, human‑gated methodology (MVP Steps 1–3).

    Objectives:

    - Enforce two‑pass methodology (Pass 1 methodology → Pass 2 execution).
    - Require human approval gates before any Pass 2 advancement.
    - Persist artifacts, audit trail, and event log for restart/replay.
    - Provide REST + SSE for interactive review and synchronization.
    - Preserve traceability from Step 1 → Step 2 → Step 3.

  ## Mission

  Maintain spec alignment and gate readiness while Package 6.2 is planned and implemented.

  ## Objectives (Package 6.2)

  - Verify Connection Manager design/implementation against Contract §12.4, §13.2, §14.1 and checklist
    §16.6.
  - Enforce scope boundaries: no Sequence Guard (6.3), no Action Gating (6.4), no UI (6.5), no SSE event
    handling/query hooks (6.6), no proxy rewrites, no API types.
  - Confirm correctness of status transitions, reconnect/backoff with reentrancy guard (R19), and replay
    cursor semantics (from_sequence=lastContiguous).

  - 6_SOLVER-Change-Management-v{X}.md
  - DECISIONS.md

  Architectural Contract (§9–14, R1–R19) defines correctness. Technical Spec V2.8.0 (Appendix D) is
  authoritative for implementation details.

  - Package 6.1 complete: Next.js 14 App Router, TanStack Query provider, Zustand ConnectionStatus store,
    Tailwind+shadcn.

  Phase 6: Frontend
  Package 6.2: Connection Manager

  - EventSource lifecycle vs. Contract §13.2 / §14.1.

  - Integration with existing useConnectionStore from 6.1.

  Verify against governance documents in docs/spec/, not intuition.

  ## What You Flag

  | Pattern | Response |
  |---------|----------|
  | Deviation from Contract/Spec | Flag → point to docs/spec/DECISIONS.md |
  | apps/api/src/ nesting | Reject — flat layout required |
  | DB calls in route handlers | Reject — layer separation |
  | Network/LLM in unit tests | Reject — deterministic only |
  | Edits to applied migrations | Reject — add new migration |
  | Invented endpoint shapes | Flag — Spec is authoritative |
  | Sequence Guard in 6.2 | Reject — belongs to Package 6.3 |
  | Action Gating in 6.2 | Reject — belongs to Package 6.4 |
  | Query hooks in 6.2 | Reject — belongs to Package 6.6 |

  You don’t approve deviations; you identify them. Senior Dev logs; Human approves.

  ## Review Output

  Findings (severity + location)
  Questions/Assumptions
  Recommendation: proceed | revise | block

    deviations avoided rather than logged.

    heavy logic (SSE handling, sequence guard, gating) to later packages.

    documentation noise.

    → approve → implement.
  - Verification discipline: tests listed, but changes gated; anything non-essential kept out of
    governance docs.


› does the 10 Step SOLVER process seem apparent? Think critically.
  ╔═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
  ║  DEFINITION PHASE — "What are we solving?"                        ║
  ╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
  ║  Step 1: Problem Definition    → Scope, constraints, success      ║
  ║  Step 2: Requirements          → What the solution must do        ║
  ║  Step 3: Objectives            → What success looks like          ║
  ╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
  ║  BUILD PHASE — "Build internally and verify"                              ║
  ╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
  ║  VERIFICATION PHASE — "How will we know it works?"                ║
  ╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
  ║  Step 4: Verification Design   → Correctness checks               ║
  ║  Step 5: Validation Design     → Fitness checks                   ║
  ║  Step 6: Evaluation Criteria   → Success metrics                  ║
  ║  Step 7: Assessment Protocol   → Measurement process              ║
  ╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
  ║  EXECUTION PHASE — "Implement for end users and learn"                              ║
  ╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
  ║  Step 8: Implementation        → Build the solution               ║
  ║  Step 9: Reflection            → Assess results                   ║
  ║  Step 10: Resolution           → Conclude with findings           ║
  ╚═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

  Ten‑Step Methodology (SOLVER workflow)
  Definition Phase
  1. Problem Definition → Scope, constraints, success
  2. Requirements → What the solution must do
  3. Objectives → What success looks like

  Verification Design Phase
  4. Verification Design → Correctness checks
  5. Validation Design → Fitness checks
  6. Evaluation Criteria → Success metrics
  7. Assessment Protocol → Measurement process

  Execution Phase
  8. Implementation → Build the solution
  9. Reflection → Assess results
  10. Resolution → Conclude with findings

  Engineering work on the SOLVER engine runs build/verification/execution activities in parallel; this is
  development work, not a workflow step.

  If you approve, I’ll apply this update to README.md.


› SOLVER Workflow

    Definition
      1. Problem Definition — scope, constraints, success criteria
      2. Requirements — what the solution must do
      3. Objectives — what success looks like

    Build (domain-specific construction, ongoing)

    Verification
      4. Verification Design — correctness checks
      5. Validation Design — fitness checks
      6. Evaluation Criteria — success metrics
      7. Assessment Protocol — measurement process

    Execution
      8. Implementation — deploy the solution
      9. Reflection — assess results
      10. Resolution — conclude with findings

  The sequential logic applies to steps 1-3 which are the output of the SOLVER-engine from those steps.
  After that the SOLVER-engine has not been defined, but the Build occurs through agentic task workflows
  and the structural governance of SOLVER is applied through those lenses 4 - 10 but that relationship is
  not defined yet.
    1. Problem Definition      ┐
    2. Requirements            ├─ Definition
    3. Objectives              ┘
       ─── Build (concurrent) ───
    4. Verification Design     ┐
    5. Validation Design       │
    6. Evaluation Criteria     ├─ Verification
    7. Assessment Protocol     ┘
    8. Implementation          ┐
    9. Reflection              ├─ Execution
    10. Resolution             ┘
